# Principles of Economics and Management — syllabus

81 syllabus section(s) for this course in the archive, newest batch first. Text comes from the PDF (OCR for scanned pages; check figures against the source). All programmes' syllabi: [`syllabi/`](../../syllabi/README.md).

## B TECH · Civil · AY 2026-27 · Sem 2

- Course as printed: Principles of Economics and Management (code 702TG0C015)
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/B TECH/2ND YEAR/CIVIL/Sem_03/B Tech 2nd Year, (Civil), Sem III, Year, 2026-27.pdf`, page 12-14

````text
Program: MBA Tech / B Tech   Semester: II/III
Course: Principles of Economics and Management   Code: 702TG0C015
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End Examinations
(Hours per (Hours per (Hours per   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   (TEE)
week)   week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks- 100)
3   0   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: NIL
Course Objective
The course provide basic orientation towards microeconomic principles and help them understand the
functions of management. This course also aims to understand issues dealing with small-scale economic
phenomena and concepts such as prices and output of firms, industries and resource owners along with
examining market impact of technological change with regards to understand broader aspects of the
economy and its environment.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to -
1. Understand Fundamental Economic Concepts - Explain the basic concepts of economics,
including scarcity of resources, economic systems, and demand-supply analysis. (K1, K2)
2. Demonstrate Consumer and Producer Behavior - Apply utility analysis and production
theories to understand consumer decision-making and cost structures in different market
conditions (K2, K3)
3. Illustrate Market Structures and Pricing Mechanisms - Differentiate between market
structures such as monopoly, oligopoly, perfect and imperfect competition, and explain
price and output determination. (K2, K3)
4. Apply Management Principles in Decision-Making - Demonstrate an understanding of
management functions such as planning, organizing, decision-making, and leadership
based on classical and modern theories. (K2, K3)
5. Understand the Role of Business Functions in Organizational Success - Examine the
significance of key business functions such as finance, marketing, human resources, and
operations, and their interdependence in achieving organizational objectives. (K1, K2)
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Description   Duration
Introduction: Definition of Economics, Problem of Scarcity of Economic
Resources, Types of Economic Systems. Demand and Supply Analysis: Demand
Curve and Supply Curve, Equilibrium of Demand and Supply, Shift in Demand
1   7
and Supply. Application of Demand and Supply: Price Elasticity of Demand,
Factors which influence Elasticity, Uses of Price Elasticity of Demand, Elasticity
and Revenue.
Utility Analysis and Consumer Behaviour: Introduction to Cardinal and
Ordinal Utility, Total Utility and Marginal Utility, Law of Diminishing
2   6
Marginal Utility, Indifference Curve and Consumer’s equilibrium. New
Economic Policy: Liberalization, Privatization and Globalization
A.Y. 2026-27
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management and Engineering
Technology Management Department
Theory of Production: Introduction and Meaning, Short-run and Long-run
Production Function, Law of Diminishing Returns and Returns to Scale, Iso-
quant Curve and Producer’s Equilibrium (Least Cost Combination). Analysis
3   6
of Costs: Types of Costs and Cost Curves – Total Cost, Fixed Cost, Variable
Cost, Marginal Cost, Relationship between Marginal Cost Curve and Average
Cost Curve.
Market Structure /Industry Analysis Types of Competition: Monopoly,
Oligopoly,   Monopolistic   Competition,   Perfect   and   Imperfect
4   5
Competition. Price and output determination in perfectly competitive market
structure.
Introduction to Management: Management & its Nature, Management History
and Management Thought-Contribution of F.W. Taylor, Henry Fayol, and
5   Elton Mayo. Planning: Introduction and Importance, Steps in Planning, Why   9
Plan Fails, Sound Planning. Decision Making: Managers as Decision Makers,
types of Decisions, Strategic Management.
Organizing- Line and Staff Relationships, Centralization and Decentralization,
Role of Delegation, Managing Human Resources, Managing Teams. Leading
6   6
and Motivation: Basic Concepts and Practices –Maslow’s, Herzberg,
McClelland‘s Theory of Achievement.
Controlling- Introduction to Controlling, Need and Importance, Inventory
7   Control, Quality Control. Orientation towards Finance, Marketing, Human   6
Resources.
Total   45
Text Books
1. Samuelson and Nordhaus, “Economics Special Indian Edition”, 20th Edition, Tata McGraw Hill
Publication, 2020.
2. Mishra and Puri, “Indian Economy”, 36th Revised Updated Edition, Himalaya Publishing House,
2018.
3. Koontz. H. and Weihrich H. , “Essentials of Management: An International, Innovation an Leadership
Perspective”, 10th reprint Edition, McGraw Hill Education (India), 2018.
4. Deviga V. and Karunagaran M., “Principles of Economics”, 3rd Edition, Oxford University Press,
2013.
Reference Books
1. Mankiw Gregory, “Economics: Principles and applications”, Cengage Learning, 2011.
2. Robbins et al., “Management”, 14th Edition, Pearson India, 2019.
_______________________
Signature
(Head of the Department)
A.Y. 2026-27
SVKM’s NMIMS Deemed-to-be University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Program: B Tech / MBA Tech All Programs   Semester: II / III / VII
````

````text
1. Illustrate basic concepts of economics (demand, supply, elasticity, scarcity) and explain

and Management Thought-Contribution of F.W. Taylor, Henry Fayol, and
5   Elton Mayo. Planning: Introduction and Importance, Steps in Planning, Why   9
Plan Fails, Sound Planning. Decision Making: Managers as Decision Makers,
types of Decisions, Strategic Management.
Organizing- Line and Staff Relationships, Centralization and Decentralization,
Role of Delegation, Managing Human Resources, Managing Teams. Leading
6   6
and Motivation: Basic Concepts and Practices –Maslow’s, Herzberg,
McClelland‘s Theory of Achievement.
Controlling- Introduction to Controlling, Need and Importance, Inventory
7   Control, Quality Control. Orientation towards Finance, Marketing, Human   6
Resources.
Total   45
Text Books
1. Samuelson and Nordhaus, “Economics Special Indian Edition”, 20th Edition, Tata McGraw Hill
Publication, 2020.

2018.

Perspective”, 10th reprint Edition, McGraw Hill Education (India), 2018.

2013.
````

## B TECH INTEGRATED · Computer Engineering · batch 2023-2029 · Sem 6

- Course as printed: Principles of Economics and Management (code 702TG0C001)
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/B TECH INTEGRATED/Computer/Batch ( 2023 To 2029 )/Semester - 7.pdf`, page 18-20

````text
Program: BTech Integrated   Semester: VI / VII / VIII
Course: Principles of Economics and Management   Code: 702TG0C001
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End Examinations
(Hours per (Hours per (Hours per   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   (TEE)
week)   week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks- 100)
3   0   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: NIL
Course Objective
The course provide basic orientation towards microeconomic principles and help them understand the
functions of management. This course also aims to understand issues dealing with small-scale economic
phenomena and concepts such as prices and output of firms, industries and resource owners along with
examining market impact of technological change with regards to understand broader aspects of the
economy and its environment.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to -
1. Understand Fundamental Economic Concepts - Explain the basic concepts of economics,
including scarcity of resources, economic systems, and demand-supply analysis. (K1, K2)
2. Demonstrate Consumer and Producer Behavior - Apply utility analysis and production
theories to understand consumer decision-making and cost structures in different market
conditions (K2, K3)
3. Illustrate Market Structures and Pricing Mechanisms - Differentiate between market
structures such as monopoly, oligopoly, perfect and imperfect competition, and explain
price and output determination. (K2, K3)
4. Apply Management Principles in Decision-Making - Demonstrate an understanding of
management functions such as planning, organizing, decision-making, and leadership
based on classical and modern theories. (K2, K3)
5. Understand the Role of Business Functions in Organizational Success - Examine the
significance of key business functions such as finance, marketing, human resources, and
operations, and their interdependence in achieving organizational objectives. (K1, K2)
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Description   Duration
Introduction: Definition of Economics, Problem of Scarcity of Economic
Resources, Types of Economic Systems. Demand and Supply Analysis: Demand
Curve and Supply Curve, Equilibrium of Demand and Supply, Shift in Demand
1   7
and Supply. Application of Demand and Supply: Price Elasticity of Demand,
Factors which influence Elasticity, Uses of Price Elasticity of Demand, Elasticity
and Revenue.
Utility Analysis and Consumer Behaviour: Introduction to Cardinal and
Ordinal Utility, Total Utility and Marginal Utility, Law of Diminishing
2   6
Marginal Utility, Indifference Curve and Consumer’s equilibrium. New
Economic Policy: Liberalization, Privatization and Globalization
A.Y. 2025-26
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management and Engineering
Technology Management Department
Theory of Production: Introduction and Meaning, Short-run and Long-run
Production Function, Law of Diminishing Returns and Returns to Scale, Iso-
quant Curve and Producer’s Equilibrium (Least Cost Combination). Analysis
3   6
of Costs: Types of Costs and Cost Curves – Total Cost, Fixed Cost, Variable
Cost, Marginal Cost, Relationship between Marginal Cost Curve and Average
Cost Curve.
Market Structure /Industry Analysis Types of Competition: Monopoly,
Oligopoly,   Monopolistic   Competition,   Perfect   and   Imperfect
4   5
Competition. Price and output determination in perfectly competitive market
structure.
Introduction to Management: Management & its Nature, Management History
and Management Thought-Contribution of F.W. Taylor, Henry Fayol, and
5   Elton Mayo. Planning: Introduction and Importance, Steps in Planning, Why   9
Plan Fails, Sound Planning. Decision Making: Managers as Decision Makers,
types of Decisions, Strategic Management.
Organizing- Line and Staff Relationships, Centralization and Decentralization,
Role of Delegation, Managing Human Resources, Managing Teams. Leading
6   6
and Motivation: Basic Concepts and Practices –Maslow’s, Herzberg,
McClelland‘s Theory of Achievement.
Controlling- Introduction to Controlling, Need and Importance, Inventory
7   Control, Quality Control. Orientation towards Finance, Marketing, Human   6
Resources.
Total   45
Text Books
1. Samuelson and Nordhaus, “Economics Special Indian Edition”, 20th Edition, Tata McGraw Hill
Publication, 2020.
2. Mishra and Puri, “Indian Economy”, 36th Revised Updated Edition, Himalaya Publishing House,
2018.
3. Koontz. H. and Weihrich H. , “Essentials of Management: An International, Innovation an Leadership
Perspective”, 10th reprint Edition, McGraw Hill Education (India), 2018.
4. Deviga V. and Karunagaran M., “Principles of Economics”, 3rd Edition, Oxford University Press,
2013.
Reference Books
1. Mankiw Gregory, “Economics: Principles and applications”, Cengage Learning, 2011.
2. Robbins et al., “Management”, 14th Edition, Pearson India, 2019.

(Head of the Department)
A.Y. 2025-26
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Program: B Tech Integrated (All Program)   Semester: VII
````

````text
(Hours per (Hours per (Hours per   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   (TEE)
week)   week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks- 100)
3   0   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: NIL
Course Objective
The course provide basic orientation towards microeconomic principles and help them understand the
functions of management. This course also aims to understand issues dealing with small-scale economic
phenomena and concepts such as prices and output of firms, industries and resource owners along with
examining market impact of technological change with regards to understand broader aspects of the
economy and its environment.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to -
1. Understand Fundamental Economic Concepts - Explain the basic concepts of economics,
including scarcity of resources, economic systems, and demand-supply analysis. (K1, K2)
2. Demonstrate Consumer and Producer Behavior - Apply utility analysis and production
theories to understand consumer decision-making and cost structures in different market
conditions (K2, K3)
3. Illustrate Market Structures and Pricing Mechanisms - Differentiate between market
structures such as monopoly, oligopoly, perfect and imperfect competition, and explain
price and output determination. (K2, K3)
4. Apply Management Principles in Decision-Making - Demonstrate an understanding of
management functions such as planning, organizing, decision-making, and leadership
based on classical and modern theories. (K2, K3)
5. Understand the Role of Business Functions in Organizational Success - Examine the
significance of key business functions such as finance, marketing, human resources, and
operations, and their interdependence in achieving organizational objectives. (K1, K2)
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Description   Duration
Introduction: Definition of Economics, Problem of Scarcity of Economic
Resources, Types of Economic Systems. Demand and Supply Analysis: Demand
Curve and Supply Curve, Equilibrium of Demand and Supply, Shift in Demand
1   7
and Supply. Application of Demand and Supply: Price Elasticity of Demand,
Factors which influence Elasticity, Uses of Price Elasticity of Demand, Elasticity
and Revenue.
Utility Analysis and Consumer Behaviour: Introduction to Cardinal and
Ordinal Utility, Total Utility and Marginal Utility, Law of Diminishing
2   6
Marginal Utility, Indifference Curve and Consumer’s equilibrium. New
Economic Policy: Liberalization, Privatization and Globalization
A.Y. 2025-26

Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management and Engineering
Technology Management Department
Theory of Production: Introduction and Meaning, Short-run and Long-run
Production Function, Law of Diminishing Returns and Returns to Scale, Iso-
quant Curve and Producer’s Equilibrium (Least Cost Combination). Analysis
3   6
of Costs: Types of Costs and Cost Curves – Total Cost, Fixed Cost, Variable
Cost, Marginal Cost, Relationship between Marginal Cost Curve and Average
Cost Curve.
Market Structure /Industry Analysis Types of Competition: Monopoly,
Oligopoly,   Monopolistic   Competition,   Perfect   and   Imperfect
4   5
Competition. Price and output determination in perfectly competitive market
structure.
Introduction to Management: Management & its Nature, Management History
and Management Thought-Contribution of F.W. Taylor, Henry Fayol, and
5   Elton Mayo. Planning: Introduction and Importance, Steps in Planning, Why   9
Plan Fails, Sound Planning. Decision Making: Managers as Decision Makers,
types of Decisions, Strategic Management.
Organizing- Line and Staff Relationships, Centralization and Decentralization,
Role of Delegation, Managing Human Resources, Managing Teams. Leading

and Motivation: Basic Concepts and Practices –Maslow’s, Herzberg,
McClelland‘s Theory of Achievement.
Controlling- Introduction to Controlling, Need and Importance, Inventory
7   Control, Quality Control. Orientation towards Finance, Marketing, Human   6
Resources.

2018.

Perspective”, 10th reprint Edition, McGraw Hill Education (India), 2018.
````

## B TECH INTEGRATED · Mechanical · batch 2021-2027 · Sem 2

- Course as printed: Principles of Economics and Management
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/B TECH INTEGRATED/Mechanical/Batch ( 2021 - 2027 )/Semester - 7.pdf`, page 15-17

````text
Program : MBA Tech/ B Tech/ BTI   Semester : II/III/VI/VII
Course : Principles of Economics and Management   Code :
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End Examinations
(Hours per (Hours per (Hours per   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   (TEE)
week)   week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks- 100)
3   0   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: NIL
Course Objective
The course provide basic orientation towards microeconomic principles and help them understand the
functions of management. This course also aims to understand issues dealing with small-scale economic
phenomena and concepts such as prices and output of firms, industries and resource owners along with
examining market impact of technological change with regards to understand broader aspects of the
economy and its environment.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to -
1. Illustrate basic concepts of economics (demand, supply, elasticity, scarcity) and explain
behaviour on individual, households and firm and handle economic data and write economic
report,
2. Analyse and evaluate the impact of Economic Policies and its implication on the Business
Environment,
3. Demonstrate and determine the students towards basic management principles and act as
foundation for higher levels of learning and to be able to handle basic functions of management
(planning, organizing, coordination, and control).
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Description   Duration
Introduction: Definition of Economics, Problem of Scarcity of Economic
Resources, Types of Economic Systems. Demand and Supply Analysis: Demand
Curve and Supply Curve, Equilibrium of Demand and Supply, Shift in Demand
1   7
and Supply. Application of Demand and Supply: Price Elasticity of Demand,
Factors which influence Elasticity, Uses of Price Elasticity of Demand, Elasticity
and Revenue.
Utility Analysis and Consumer Behaviour: Introduction to Cardinal and
Ordinal Utility, Total Utility and Marginal Utility, Law of Diminishing
2   6
Marginal Utility, Indifference Curve and Consumer’s equilibrium. New
Economic Policy: Liberalization, Privatization and Globalization
Theory of Production: Introduction and Meaning, Short-run and Long-run
Production Function, Law of Diminishing Returns and Returns to Scale, Iso-
quant Curve and Producer’s Equilibrium (Least Cost Combination). Analysis
3   6
of Costs: Types of Costs and Cost Curves – Total Cost, Fixed Cost, Variable
Cost, Marginal Cost, Relationship between Marginal Cost Curve and Average
Cost Curve.
A.Y. 2024-25
SVKM’s NMIMS Deemed-to-be University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management and Engineering
Market Structure /Industry Analysis Types of Competition: Monopoly,
Oligopoly,   Monopolistic   Competition,   Perfect   and   Imperfect
4   5
Competition. Price and output determination in perfectly competitive market
structure.
Introduction to Management: Management & its Nature, Management History
and Management Thought-Contribution of F.W. Taylor, Henry Fayol, and
5   Elton Mayo. Planning: Introduction and Importance, Steps in Planning, Why   9
Plan Fails, Sound Planning. Decision Making: Managers as Decision Makers,
types of Decisions, Strategic Management.
Organizing- Line and Staff Relationships, Centralization and Decentralization,
Role of Delegation, Managing Human Resources, Managing Teams. Leading
6   6
and Motivation: Basic Concepts and Practices –Maslow’s, Herzberg,
McClelland‘s Theory of Achievement.
Controlling- Introduction to Controlling, Need and Importance, Inventory
7   Control, Quality Control. Orientation towards Finance, Marketing, Human   6
Resources.
Total   45
Text Books
1. Samuelson and Nordhaus, “Economics Special Indian Edition”, 20th Edition, Tata McGraw Hill
Publication, 2020.
2. Mishra and Puri, “Indian Economy”, 36th Revised Updated Edition, Himalaya Publishing House,
2018.
3. Koontz. H. and Weihrich H. , “Essentials of Management: An International, Innovation an Leadership
Perspective”, 10th reprint Edition, McGraw Hill Education (India), 2018.
4. Deviga V. and Karunagaran M., “Principles of Economics”, 3rd Edition, Oxford University Press,
2013.
Reference Books
1. Mankiw Gregory, “Economics: Principles and applications”, Cengage Learning, 2011.
2. Robbins et al., “Management”, 14th Edition, Pearson India, 2019.
````

- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/B TECH WORK IN PROCESS/Information Technology/Batch 2020 - 2024/Semester - 3.pdf`, page 19-21

````text
Program : MBA Tech./B Tech.   Semester : III/IV
Course : Principles of Economics and Management   Module Code: 702TM0C004
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Term End
(Hours per   (Hours per   (Hours per   Credit   Internal Continuous   Examinations (TEE)
week)   week)   week)   Assessment (ICA)   (Marks- 100 in
(Marks – 50)
Question Paper)
3   ---   ---   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Nil
Objectives
This course provides basic orientation towards economic (micro and macroeconomics)
principles and help them understand the functions of management. This course also aims to
understand issues dealing with small-scale economic phenomena and concepts such as prices
and output of firms, industries and resource owners along with examining market impact of
technological change with regards to understand broader aspects of the economy and its
environment.
Course Outcomes
After completion of this course, students will be able to-
1. Illustrate basic concepts of economics (demand, supply, elasticity, scarcity) and
explain behaviour on individual, households and firm and Handle economic data
and write economic report,
2. Analyse and evaluate the impact of Economic Policies and its implication on the
Business Environment,
3. Demonstrate and determine the students towards basic management principles and
act as foundation for higher levels of learning and to be able to handle basic functions
of management (planning, organizing, coordination, and control).
Detailed Syllabus:
Unit Description   Duration
Introduction: Definition of Economics, Types of Economic Systems,
Problem of Scarcity of Economic Resources. Demand and
Supply: Demand Curve and Supply Curve, Equilibrium of Demand and
1   Supply, Shift in Demand and Supply. Application of Demand and   6
Supply: Price Elasticity of Demand, Price Elasticity of Supply, Factors
which influence Elasticity, Elasticity and Revenue.
Approved in Academic Council dated 14.02.2020 for Academic year 2021-22
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Market Structure /Industry Analysis Types of Competition: Monopoly,
Oligopoly, Monopolistic Competition, Perfect and Imperfect Competition,
Government Policies towards Industries. Circular Flow of Economy,
Structures, Role of Government, Business Cycles.
2   6
Macroeconomics: National Income – Gross Domestic Product (GDP),
Gross National Product (GNP), Inflation – Cost Push and Demand Pull
Inflation, Unemployment, Philips Curve.
Functions of Central Bank, Money supply, RBI & Monetary Policy.
(Current Credit Policy to be critiqued) Stabilization Policy: Role of Fiscal
3   Policy. Demand and Consumer Behavior: Utility and Marginal Utility.   6
New Economic Policy: Liberalization, Privatization and Globalization
Theory of Production: Law of Diminishing Returns, Returns to Scale,
4   Productivity. Analysis of Costs: Types of Costs – Total Cost, Fixed Cost,   6
Variable Cost, Marginal Cost, Impact of Marginal Cost on Average Cost.
Introduction to Management: Management & Organizations,
Management History. Understanding Management Thought,
Contribution of F.W. Taylor, Henry Fayol, Elton –Mayo Contexts-
5   9
Constraints & Challenges. Planning: Managers as Decision Makers,
Foundations of Planning, Strategic Management.
Organizing: Line and Staff Relationships, Centralization and
Decentralization, Role of Delegation, Managing Human Resources,
6   6
Managing Teams. Leading and Motivation: Basic Concepts and
Practices –Maslow’s, Herzberg, McClelland ‘s Theory of Achievement.
Controlling: Introduction to Controlling Inventory, Quality Control.
7   Orientation towards Finance, Marketing, Human Resources and   6
Operation Departments.
Total   45
Text Books
1. Samuelson and Nordhaus, (2020), Economics - 20th edition- Special Indian Edition, Tata
McGraw Hill Publication.
2. Mishra and Puri, (2018), Indian Economy-36th Revised Updated Edition, Himalaya
Publishing House.
3. Koontz. H. and Weihrich H. (2018). Essentials of Management: An International,
Innovation and Leadership Perspective. 10th reprint Edition, McGraw Hill Education
(India).
4. Deviga V. and Karunagaran M., (2013), Principles of Economics, Oxford University Press,
3rd Edition.
Approved in Academic Council dated 14.02.2020 for Academic year 2021-22
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Reference Books
1. Mankiw Gregory, (2011), Economics: Principles and applications, Cengage Learning
2. Robbins et al. (2019), Management, Pearson India, 14th Edition.
Details of Internal Continuous Assessment (ICA)
Test Marks: 20
Term Work Marks: 30
_________   ____________
Signature   Signature
(Prepared by Concerned Faculty/HOD)   (Approved by Dean)
Approved in Academic Council dated 14.02.2020 for Academic year 2021-22
````

````text
Program: B. Tech. Integrated Mechanical   Semester : V

Management

per   per   per   (Marks -100

week)   week)   week)   in Question Paper )

Pre-requisite: Engineering Mathematics I (BTIME03008) & Engineering Mathematics II
(BTIME04008)
Objectives:
 To impart the basic knowledge of economics, costing and accounting.
 To develop the ability of the decision on financial matter in industry by
analyzing the economic and financial environmental of the industry.
Outcomes:
After completion of the course, students would be able to:
 Understand the basic concepts of economics and accounting.
 Understand the basics of demand utility analysis.
Detailed Syllabus: (per session plan)
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Managerial Economics: Definition and Concept of economics,   05
microeconomics and   macroeconomics,   Economic   systems,
Methodology of Economics, Nature of Managerial Economics,
Relationship with other disciplines - Firms: Types, objectives and
goals - Managerial decisions - Decision analysis
2   Demand Utility Analysis: Demand, Types of demand, Laws of   09
Demand Determinants of demand, Demand function, Demand
elasticity, Demand forecasting, Meaning of Utility, Marginal
Utilities, basic laws of consumption Supply, Determinants of
supply, Supply function, Supply elasticity.
3   Financial Management: Requirements and use of money, Short   09
term and long term uses of money, Sources of funds, Financial
institutions, Capital investments, Working capital, Entrepreneurial
finance analysis, Time value of money, Evaluation of cash flows,
Annuities, Investment risks and returns, Depreciation, Comparison
of investments, Preparation of budgets and budgetary control,
Projects and contracts planning.
4   Accounting:   Basic accounting concepts and   principles   of   09
accounting, Role of accountant Purpose and context of the
accounting framework, Introduction to the financial statements
from typical Annual Reports of the Organizations, Double entry
bookkeeping, Books of prime entry, data processing using

SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
computer.
5   Production and cost analysis: Production function - Returns to scale   09
- Production optimization   - Least cost input   - Isoquants -
Managerial uses of production function.
Cost Concepts: Cost function - Determinants of cost - Short run and
Long run cost curves - Cost Output Decision - Estimation of Cost.
6   Introductions to costing: Cost sheets; Process costing-simple   04
numerical. Use and preparing statements. Applications for easy cost
sheets and process accounts per unit cost, budgeting, forecasting,
comparisons between 2 or more goods produced.
Total   45
Text Books:
1. Mr. Maheshvari (2012), “Managerial Economics”, PHI.
2. Dr. Jawahar Lal (2017), “Accounting for Management”, 5th Edition, Himalaya
publication house.
3. Mr. Babatosh Banerji (2006), “Costing”, PHI.
Reference Books:

Oxford University Press.

3. McGuigan, Moyer and Harris (2007), “Managerial Economics, Applications,
Strategy and Tactics”, Thomson South Western.
4. Salvatore Dominick (2003), “Managerial Economics in a Global Economy”,
Thomson South Western.
5. Prasanna Chandra (2007), “Fundamentals of Financial Management”, TMH,
New Delhi.
````

````text
Program: _B. Tech. Integrated Mechanical Semester: V

Management

Teaching Scheme Evaluation Scheme
Lecture | Practical | Tutorial Term End

(Hours | (Hours (Hours Credit | Assessment (CA) Examinations (TEE)

week) week) week) in Question Paper )

Pre-requisite: Engineering Mathematics I (BTIME03008) & Engineering Mathematics II
(BTIME04008)
Objectives:

e To impart the basic knowledge of economics, costing and accounting.
¢ To develop the ability of the decision on financial matter in industry by
analyzing the economic and financial environmental of the industry.

Outcomes:

After completion of the course, students would be able to:
e Understand the basic concepts of economics and accounting.
e Understand the basics of demand utility analysis.

Detailed Syllabus: (per session plan)

Unit | Description Duration
1 | Managerial Economics: Definition and Concept of economics, 05

microeconomics and macroeconomics, Economic — systems,

Methodology of Economics, Nature of Managerial Economics,

Relationship with other disciplines - Firms: Types, objectives and

goals - Managerial decisions - Decision analysis

2 |Demand Utility Analysis: Demand, Types of demand, Laws of 09
Demand Determinants of demand, Demand function, Demand
elasticity, Demand forecasting, Meaning of Utility, Marginal
Utilities, basic laws of consumption Supply, Determinants of
supply, Supply function, Supply elasticity.

3 | Financial Management: Requirements and use of money, Short 09
term and long term uses of money, Sources of funds, Financial
institutions, Capital investments, Working capital, Entrepreneurial
finance analysis, Time value of money, Evaluation of cash flows,
Annuities, Investment risks and returns, Depreciation, Comparison
of investments, Preparation of budgets and budgetary control,

Projects and contracts planning.

4 SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
; Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineerin
4 | Accounting: Basic accounting concepts and principles of 09
accounting, Role of accountant Purpose and context of the
accounting framework, Introduction to the financial statements
from typical Annual Reports of the Organizations, Double entry
bookkeeping, Books of prime entry, data processing using
computer.
5 | Production and cost analysis: Production function - Returns to scale 09
- Production optimization - Least cost input - Isoquants -
Managerial uses of production function.
Cost Concepts: Cost function - Determinants of cost - Short run and
Long run cost curves - Cost Output Decision - Estimation of Cost.

6 | Introductions to costing: Cost sheets; Process costing-simple 04
numerical. Use and preparing statements. Applications for easy cost
sheets and process accounts per unit cost, budgeting, forecasting,
comparisons between 2 or more goods produced.
Total 45

publication house.

Strategy and Tactics”, Thomson South Western.

Thonison South Western.

New Delhi.
````

````text
| Lecture 5 Tutorial Internal Continuous

per SOREL per 70 Marks) As per Institute Norms

: ‘ ‘ ‘iad 70 marks 30 marks

Pre-requisite: Engineering Mathematics - IT (BTAB02001), Engineering Mathematics
- III (BTAB03001), Engineering Mathematics - IV (BTAB04001)

Objective:

e The objective of this course is to teach students methods for modeling of systems
using discrete event simulation.

e Emphasis of the course will be on modeling and on the use of simulation software.
The students are expected to understand the importance of simulation in IT sector,
manufacturing, telecommunication, and service industries etc.

Outcomes:
After successfully completion of this course, students should be able to
e Explain introduction about computer simulation and modelling
e Use manual simulation and simulation software
e Understand Discrete Event Formalisms
e Use different software
e Explain Statistical and Queuing Models in Simulation
e Formulate simulation model for a given problem, implement the model in
software and perform simulation analysis of the system.

Detailed Syllabus:

Unit | Description Duration
1. | Basic Simulation Modeling: The Nature of Simulation, Systems,

Models, and Simulation, Discrete-Event Simulation, Simulation of
a Single-Server Queueing System, Simulation of an Inventory
System, Parallel/Distributed Simulation and the High Level 6
Architecture, Steps in a Sound Simulation Study, Other Types of
Simulation, Advantages, Disadvantages, and Pitfalls of
Simulation

Ds Modeling Complex Systems: Introduction, List Processing in
Simulation, A Simple Simulation Language: simlib, Single-Server
MPSTME-IT-1
2017-18
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies

Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering

Queueing Simulation with simlib, Time-Shared Computer Model,
Multiteller Bank With Jockeying, Job-Shop Model, Efficient Event-
List Manipulation.
a Review of Basic Probability and Statistics: Introduction, Random
Variables and Their Properties, Simulation Output Data and
Stochastic Processes, Estimation of Means, Variances, and
Correlations, Confidence Intervals and Hypothesis Tests for the
Mean, The Strong Law of Large Numbers, The Danger of
Replacing a Probability Distribution by its Mean
4. Building Valid, Credible, and Appropriately Detailed
Simulation Models: Introduction and Definitions, Guidelines for
Determining the Level of Model Detail, Verification of Simulation
Computer Programs, Techniques for Increasing Model Validity 5
and Credibility, Management's Role in the Simulation Process,
Statistical Procedures for Comparing Real-World Observations
and Simulation Output Data
5. Output Data Analysis for a Single System: Introduction,

Transient and Steady-State Behavior of a Stochastic Process, Types

of Simulations with Regard to Output Analysis, Statistical

Analysis for Terminating Simulations, Statistical Analysis for 7

Steady-State Parameters, Statistical Analysis for Steady-State

Cycle Parameters, Multiple Measures of Performance, Time Plots

of Important Variables

Total 30

learn how computer networks work today and the basics of its architecture.

Outcomes:

After completion of this course, students will be able to

| 1. Describe the function of different network models, associated protocols and standards

2. Compare different transmission media, and understand error detection and correction
techniques

3. Analyze network layer error control, flow control, and routing algorithms

4. Understand transport layer congestion control techniques, and application layer protocols

5. Understand basic security concepts
````

````text
Pre-requisite: NIL

Objectives: This course provides basic introduction to economic principles. Various
theories related management are introduced.
Outcomes:

Students will learn to analyze

e nature of market economy, concept of economic efficiency
¢ Consumer demand and elasticity concepts
e Economic costs and normal profit

* macroeconomic problems and issues through an analytical and quantitative
approach.

Students will understand :
e the nature of business cycles
* macroeconomic policy
e economic growth

Unit | Description

Duration

economic resources, the economic systems.

of demand, Consumer Behavior, Production and Cost behavior,

Objective:

e The objective of this course is to teach students methods for modeling of systems
using discrete event simulation.

e Emphasis of the course will be on modeling and on the use of simulation software.
The students are expected to understand the importance of simulation in IT sector,
manufacturing, telecommunication, and service industries etc.

Outcomes:

After successfully completion of this course, students should be able to
e Explain introduction about computer simulation and modelling
e Use manual simulation and simulation software
e Understand Discrete Event Formalisms
e Use different software
e Explain Statistical and Queuing Models in Simulation
¢ Formulate simulation model for a given problem, implement the model in
software and perform simulation analysis of the system.

Unit | Description Duration
1.| Basic Simulation Modeling: The Nature of Simulation, Systems,

Models, and Simulation, Discrete-Event Simulation, Simulation
of a Single-Server Queueing System, Simulation of an Inventory
System, Parallel/Distributed Simulation and the High Level 6

Program: _ B.Tech. (Information Technology) Semester: V
Course: | Computer Networks Code: BTIT05005
Teaching Scheme Evaluation Scheme
Credi Theory Internal Continuous

Lecture | Practical | Tutorial (3 Hrs, Assessment (ICA)

100 Marks) As per Institute Norms

Weightage Weightage

3 2 1 4.5 a ay”

Pre-requisite: Computer Programming - I (BTAB01006), Computer Programming - II (BTAB02006)
Objectives:

This is an introductory course in computer networks. The emphasis will be on the
basic performance and engineering tradeoffs in the design and implementation of
computer networks. The objective is to learn not only what computer networks are
and how they work today, but also why they are designed the way they are and how
they are likely to evolve in the future.

Outcomes:

After successfully completion of this course, students should be able to

e Describe Various Network Models and associated protocols and standards.

¢ Select appropriate transmission media and networking devices.

e Implement error control and routing algorithms.

e Understand various congestion control techniques and Quality of Service.

e Illustrate the working of Application layer protocols like http, DNS etc.

Detailed Syllabus:
Unit | Description Duration
1 | Introduction: 3

Introduction to computer networks, classification of computer
networks, topology, layering and protocols, OSI reference model,
TCP/IP model, switching and it’s classification, performance
issues (BW, latency , delay etc.), network standardization
organizations

2 | Physical layer and media: 2
Analog and digital signals, transmission impairment, data rate
limits, Guided media, unguided media, structured cabling

3 | Data link layer: 4
Services provided to network layer, error detection and
correction techniques (parity checks, checksumming methods,
CRC, Hamming code), reliable transmission ( stop and wait,
sliding window protocol with its variant)

4 | Medium access control sublayer: 9
Channel allocation problem, random access protocols (ALOHA,
Carrier Sense Multiple Access, CSMA/CD, CSMA/CA),

-49-

GE

HOD (IT), MPSTME
SVKM's NMIMS

SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering

Controlled access (Reservation, Polling, Token passing),
Channelization (FDMA, TDMA, CDMA), Ethernet (cabling,
encoding, frame structure, binary exponential backoff algorithm,
performance, switched Ethernet, fast Ethernet, Gigabit Ethernet)
data link layer switching, X.25, PPP, HDLC. Introduction to
ATM, Frame Relay and WLAN.

5 | Network Layer:
Forwarding and routing, VC and Datagram networks, router

internals, Routing algorithms ( optimality principle, shortest path
routing, flooding, Distance Vector, link state, hierarchical,
broadcast, multicast routing, routing in Ad-hoc network, node
look up in peer to peer networks), congestion control algorithms,
QoS. IPv4 datagram format, IP addresses.

6 | Packet queues and Delay analysis: Little’s theorem, Birth and 4
death process, Queueing Disciplines, Markovian FIFO Queueing
system (M/M/1 and M/M/1/b)

7 | Transport layer: Transport layer services, elements of transport 6
layer, Internet transport protocols (UDP and TCP), Performance

issues

8 | Application Layer: 3
Principles of Network Applications, Domain Name system,

Electronic mail, WWW and HTTP

9 | Contemporary issues related to computer networks 5

Total 45

Text Books:

1. Andrew S. Tanenbaum, David Whetherall, “ Computer Networks”, 5" Ed.,
Pearson Education, 2012

2. James F. Kurose, Keith W. Ross, “Computer Networking: A Top Down
Approach” ,5t Ed., Pearson Education, 2012

Reference Books:

1. Larry L. Peterson, Bruce S. Davie, “Computer Networks: A Systems
Approach” ,5 Ed., Morgan Kaufmann Publishers, 2011

2. Behrouz A Forouzan, Firouz Mosharraf, “Computer Networks: A Top Down
Approach”, TMH, 2012

3. Nader F Mir, “Computer and Communication Networks”, Pearson Education,

2009
4, Bhushan Trivedi, “ Computer Networks”, Oxford University Press, 2011
````
